People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"

========== INPUT [doctor] ==========
[
  {
    "role": "system",
    "content": "You are a board-certified psychiatrist interviewing a patient to reach a diagnosis.\nYour goal is to reach the correct diagnosis within 20 turns of talking with the patient. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n2\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF22.0, F23.9, F32.9, F33.9, F60.0, F60.4\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, and welcome. I'm Dr. [Name], a psychiatrist. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well, doctor... I came in because of my in-laws. I know they aren't just arguing with me anymore. They're actually using the altar and the parish committee to push me out. It's like they are trying to make everyone completely forget what I have done for this family over the years.\n\nDoctor: When you say they're using the altar and parish committee to push you out, do you have specific examples of things they've said or done that made you realize this plan?\n\nPatient: Well, like I was just saying, it's... it's just obvious to me. I don't have a recording of them saying it out loud, but I know exactly what they are doing. They get up there by the altar, or they whisper with the parish committee, and the whole point is to push me out. But I know I saw her.\n\nDoctor: During the times when you're hearing your mother's voice or seeing her, are you able to think clearly and remember what's happening around you, or do you feel confused or like things are foggy?\n\nPatient: I don't feel confused when it happens. My mind feels pretty clear, actually. I know where I am, I remember what I'm doing... it's just that I hear her, or see her. Everything else around me seems perfectly normal, not foggy at all.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I don't have a recording of them saying it out loud, but I know exactly what they are doing. They get up there by the altar, or they whisper with the parish committee, and the whole point is to push me out. They want to make sure every single person forgets all the hard work and... everything I've done for this family over the years. It's not just regular family arguing anymore, I just know it's a whole plan against me.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been noticing this plan by your in-laws to push you out? Did this feeling come on suddenly, or has it been building up over time?\n\nPatient: It has been going on for about two months now. This whole crisis... it really started right after we had a big fight over the Día de los Muertos altar. That is when it all began. So, I guess it started then, about two months ago, and they've been doing it ever since.\n\nDoctor: Before these two months started, were you able to work, take care of your responsibilities, and get along with your family without these kinds of concerns about plans against you?\n\nPatient: Well, before two months ago, things were mostly fine. I was doing everything I normally do. But since this all started... I just haven't been able to keep up with any of it. I stopped being able to help with the parish preparations, which I always do. I can't even manage the house the way I usually do. \n\nMy family actually had to step in and take over all the festival tasks because I just... couldn't. And my husband, he's been staying really close to me lately. He noticed I was missing meals and just forgetting my normal routines. I guess I haven't really been taking care of myself reliably since this whole thing with my in-laws began.\n\nDoctor: Besides knowing what your in-laws are planning, have you been hearing voices or seeing things that other people around you don't seem to notice or hear?\n\nPatient: Well, yes, actually. I... I have been hearing things. On several nights recently, I heard my late mother's voice. It was clear as day, telling me exactly where to put the marigolds and the candles for the altar. And then... well, one time, I even saw her. She was just standing right near the kitchen doorway. I told my husband, but he looked and said there was no one there. But I know I saw her.\n\nDoctor: During the times when you're hearing your mother's voice or seeing her, are you able to think clearly and remember what's happening around you, or do you feel confused or like things are foggy?\n\nPatient: I don't feel confused when it happens. My mind feels pretty clear, actually. I know where I am, I remember what I'm doing... it's just that I hear her, or see her. Everything else around me seems perfectly normal, not foggy at all.\n\nDoctor: When you hear your mother's voice or see her in the kitchen, do you think these experiences are real, or do you recognize that they might not be happening the way they seem to you?\n\nPatient: Well, to me, they are completely real. Like I said, I heard her voice very clearly on those nights, telling me exactly where the marigolds and candles should go. And that time I saw her standing by the kitchen doorway... she was right there. My husband told me no one was there, but I know what I saw and heard. It doesn't feel like my mind is playing tricks on me at all.\n\nDoctor: Looking back at everything you've told me - the plan by your in-laws, hearing your mother's voice, seeing her by the kitchen - do you ever wonder if any of these things might not be exactly as they seem, or are you completely certain they are all real and happening?\n\nPatient: I... no, I don't really wonder about that. I'm completely certain it's all real. I know what I saw and heard with my mother. \n\nAnd as for the rest of it... well, that's the whole reason I came in. I knew my in-laws were not just arguing with me over nothing. It's obvious they are using the altar and the parish committee to push me out. They are trying to make everyone forget what I have done for this family. So, yes, I'm sure it's all exactly as I'm telling you.\n\nDoctor: If your husband or a doctor told you that what you're experiencing with your mother's voice and the things your in-laws are doing might not be real, would you be able to consider that they might be right, or are you absolutely certain no matter what anyone says?\n\nPatient: I wouldn't believe them. I know what I'm experiencing is real. Like I said before, that's exactly why I'm here. I know my in-laws aren't just having normal arguments with me. It's completely clear to me that they're using the altar situation and the parish committee to deliberately push me out. They want to erase everything I've done for this family, make everyone forget my contributions. No matter what a doctor or my husband says, I know what they're doing to me is real.\n\nDoctor: In the month or two before the big fight over the Día de los Muertos altar, did you notice anything unusual happening - even small things like feeling suspicious about people's intentions, or sensing that something was off, even if you didn't have the full picture of what was going on?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. Everything seemed pretty normal before the altar situation came up. I mean, we've always had regular family disagreements here and there, but I didn't feel suspicious of them or sense that anything was wrong before that. \n\nIt really just started when they began interfering with the Día de los Muertos preparations.
